you felt the need to recreate it.

The list of the items is finished, the definitions for Smart.fm are not. In
point of fact, there are exactly three areas that are not finished- the
audio for the entries, the *simplified *definitions for Smart.fm for about
70% of the items, and the adding of the items to Smart.fm with audio, which
apparently we are still not to do as Smart.fm hasn't added "Lojban" to the
language list yet.

=== 8 | 2010-03-03T23:36:05Z | Dag Odenhall <dag.odenhall@gmail.com> | bbbfec8c1003031536y43b4759ci8db672cef8166cf0@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267659365.2d371a8289029667.jbomohi:2,S
And the bash oneliner in case someone wants to do it over some time
for whatever reason:

cat gismu_places | while read v; do echo $v; espeak -vjbo --stdout
"$v" | lame -S - "valsi/$v.mp3"; done

=== 9 | 2010-03-03T23:36:01Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003031536n376e5d09r1c817a5d0b67ceb2@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267659361.01b4f8ac4966a0fb.jbomohi:2,S
On Wed, Mar 3, 2010 at 4:13 PM, Robin Lee Powell <
rlpowell@digitalkingdom.org> wrote:

> On Wed, Mar 03, 2010 at 04:10:48PM -0700, Jonathan Jones wrote:
> >    The list of the items is finished, the definitions for Smart.fm are
> not.
> >    In point of fact, there are exactly three areas that are not finished-
> the
> >    audio for the entries,
>
> Yep.
>
> > the simplified definitions for Smart.fm for about 70% of the
> > items,
>
> Trivially producable from jbofihe data.  I still want someone to
> explain to me why we're simplifying them in the first place?
>
> Oi, I've already said why. The gismu definitions need to be simplified
because, 1) The definitions from the gismu list are, more often than not,
too long for Smart.fm's cards, so they need to be shortened to fit, and 2)
They non-vocal punctuation needs to be edited out.

For this reason the definition for bakfu, which in the gismu list is "x1 is
a bundle/package/cluster/clump/pack [shape/form] containing x2, held
together by x3", becomes "x1 is a bundle or pack containing x2 held together
by x3"

The reason the sumti-fied gismu need to edited is because the phrasing
jbofi'e uses is quite often very awkward if not downright confusing. The
phrase jbofi'e returns for {lo te bakfu} is"any/some hold-er(s) of bundle",
which can easily be confused for "a person holding a bundle", which is
wrong, as the x5 of {bakfu} is "what is holding a pack together".

Quite a lot of the entries can be used almost as is, but they still need to
be gone through for that very reason.



> > and the adding of the items to Smart.fm with audio, which
> > apparently we are still not to do as Smart.fm hasn't added
> > "Lojban" to the language list yet.
>
> It's actually in the system now, just not in the right places.  :)
> I'm talking to them about it.
>
> -Robin
>
> --
> They say:  "The first AIs will be built by the military as weapons."

> other  than  the default  outcome?"  See http://shrunklink.com/cdiz

The source files are on jbovlaste. The best way to translate the dictionary is 
to get a jbovlaste account and enter definitions there.

Pierre

=== 19 | 2010-03-05T18:19:20Z | Robin Lee Powell <rlpowell@digitalkingdom.org> | 20100305181920.gq1159@digitalkingdom.org | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267813160.9b9160da97e7a3ab.jbomohi:2,S
On Fri, Mar 05, 2010 at 10:22:30AM -0400, Efrain Caro wrote:
> I tried to generate an English-Lojban dictionary. Generated it,
> did a "Save As" on the pdf link, and it told me that the webpage
> doesn't exist.

So, whenever someone does something in the definitions that LaTeX
doesn't like, the dictionary export breaks.

People *should* be testing dictionary export after all their
changes, if possible, but they don't.

English is the worst because people who have no idea what they're
doing but make jbovlaste accounts anyways are almost always working
on the English definitions.

Anyways, here's the changes I had to make to make the English export
work; except for the UTF-8 problems (the euro symbol (which I was
able to support) and the hundredth/tenth stuff (which I wasn't)), if
you made these changes please feel guilty and learn from them.  :)

I haven't updated the words yet, but give me about half an hour and
the English export should be working.

-Robin

5a6
> \usepackage{eurosym}
1221c1222
< {\sffamily\bfseries centi}\markboth{centi}{centi}\enspace {\ttfamily\bfseries[cen]}  $x_{1}$ is a hundredth [1/100; 1 ⨉ $10^{-2}$] of $x_{2}$ in dimension/aspect $x_{3}$ (default is units). (See also {grake}, {mitre}, {snidu}, {stero}, {delno}, {molro}, {kelvo}, {xampo}, {gradu}, {litce}, {merli}, {decti}, {dekto}, {femti}, {gigdo}, {gocti}, {gotro}, {kilto}, {megdo}, {mikri}, {milti}, {nanvi}, {petso}, {picti}, {terto}, {xatsi}, {xecto}, {xexso}, {zepti}, {zetro}.)
---
> {\sffamily\bfseries centi}\markboth{centi}{centi}\enspace {\ttfamily\bfseries[cen]}  $x_{1}$ is a hundredth [1/100; 1*$10^{-2}$] of $x_{2}$ in dimension/aspect $x_{3}$ (default is units). (See also {grake}, {mitre}, {snidu}, {stero}, {delno}, {molro}, {kelvo}, {xampo}, {gradu}, {litce}, {merli}, {decti}, {dekto}, {femti}, {gigdo}, {gocti}, {gotro}, {kilto}, {megdo}, {mikri}, {milti}, {nanvi}, {petso}, {picti}, {terto}, {xatsi}, {xecto}, {xexso}, {zepti}, {zetro}.)
2129c2130
< {\sffamily\bfseries daskycizborda'u}\markboth{daskycizborda'u}{daskycizborda'u} $c1=$da1 is a Pokémon/Pocket Monster of species $da2
---
> {\sffamily\bfseries daskycizborda'u}\markboth{daskycizborda'u}{daskycizborda'u} $c1=da1$ is a Pokémon/Pocket Monster of species $da2$
2197c2198

=== 21 | 2010-03-04T15:35:46Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003040735j468fb753y13666ab05fb4c612@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267716946.fe5786bcb8adcb86.jbomohi:2,S
For the gismu definitions, all the extraneous stuff (eg. anything between
any braces) to be removed, all '/' replaced with ' or ', and all variants
removed (eg. "bundle/package/cluster/clump/pack" except for the one or two
most common or most different. (so, for example, the word in the third
and/or fourth column of the gismu list, with {bakfu}, that would be 'bundle'
and 'pack', whereas with {balni}, it would be 'balcony' and 'shelf'.)
However, as Dag pointed out, the gismu definitions already have a simplified
form, so I don't think much if anything needs to be done for that.

For the sumti definitions, for lack of a better way to put it, a humanized
grammatical phrase. Some examples:
sumti jbofi'e phrase Smart.fm definition
lo bakri any/some chalk(s) a quantity of chalk
 lo se bakri any/some chalk source(s)  a source of chalk
lo te bakri any/some chalk form(s) a form of chalk

sumti jbofi'e phrase Smart.fm definition
lo cenba any/some vary-er(s) something varying
lo se cenba  any/some variant property(ies) a property of variance
lo te cenba any/some degree(s) of variation a degree of variation
lo ve cenba any/some conditions for variation a condition of varying

sumti jbofi'e phrase Smart.fm definition
lo dukse any/some excess(es)  an excess
lo se dukse any/some in excess thing(s) something in excess
lo te dukse any/some standard for excess a standard of excess

> any braces) to be removed, all '/' replaced with ' or ', and all variants
> removed (eg. "bundle/package/cluster/clump/pack" except for the one or two
> most common or most different. (so, for example, the word in the third
> and/or fourth column of the gismu list, with {bakfu}, that would be 'bundle'
> and 'pack', whereas with {balni}, it would be 'balcony' and 'shelf'.)
> However, as Dag pointed out, the gismu definitions already have a simplified
> form, so I don't think much if anything needs to be done for that.
> For the sumti definitions, for lack of a better way to put it, a humanized
> grammatical phrase. Some examples:
> sumti jbofi'e phrase Smart.fm definition
> lo bakri any/some chalk(s) a quantity of chalk
> lo se bakri any/some chalk source(s)  a source of chalk
> lo te bakri any/some chalk form(s) a form of chalk
> sumti jbofi'e phrase Smart.fm definition
> lo cenba any/some vary-er(s) something varying
> lo se cenba  any/some variant property(ies) a property of variance
> lo te cenba any/some degree(s) of variation a degree of variation
> lo ve cenba any/some conditions for variation a condition of varying
> sumti jbofi'e phrase Smart.fm definition
> lo dukse any/some excess(es) an excess
> lo se dukse any/some in excess thing(s) something in excess
> lo te dukse any/some standard for excess a standard of excess
>
> On Wed, Mar 3, 2010 at 5:09 PM, Robin Lee Powell
> <rlpowell@digitalkingdom.org> wrote:
>>
>> On Wed, Mar 03, 2010 at 04:36:01PM -0700, Jonathan Jones wrote:
>> >    On Wed, Mar 3, 2010 at 4:13 PM, Robin Lee Powell

>> >    together by x3", becomes "x1 is a bundle or pack containing x2 held

=== 23 | 2010-03-04T16:50:02Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003040850r6815c001g7fcce149a445bbe4@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267721402.e7d161cacb7ad16d.jbomohi:2,S
Not really, and the list is also a lot more difficult to edit:

From oblique keywords:
bakri1;chalk
bakri2;chalk source
bakri3;chalk form
cenba1;variable; vary
cenba2;varying property
cenba3;variance; varying amount
cenba4;variance conditions
dukse1;excess
dukse2;in excess
dukse3;excess standard
From jbofi'e, with 'any/some', '(' and ')' auto-removed:
chalks
chalk sources
chalk forms

vary-ers
variant properties
degrees of variation
excesses
in excess things
standard for excess
They're almost exactly the same, and the ones from jbofi'e don't need have a
'[gismu][#];' to remove.

=== 28 | 2010-03-05T14:33:45Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003050633o1867e02bre21166c9557a7ba9@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267799625.b34cffc1d0159b16.jbomohi:2,S
On Thu, Mar 4, 2010 at 11:07 PM, Dag Odenhall <dag.odenhall@gmail.com>wrote:

> How is "vary-ers" more natural than "variable",


It's not. It's merely "varier" spelled wrong with a '-' in it. However, it
does, IMO, have the advantage of less confusingly defining {lo cenba} than
'variable', and both are less 'natural' than 'something varying'.


> and how is "cenba1;"
> more difficult to remove than "any/some...(s)"? Easier, if anything.
>
> Because *all* the jbofi'e phrases have 'any/some' and '(s)', whereas only
one of the oblique keywords has "cenba1". To remove 'any/some' and '(s)' is
two instances of find/replace with '', to remove '[gismu][#];' is at least
one per gismu, plus one each for '1;', '2;', '3;', '4;', and '5;'.

First, I want to thank Dag and Robin for their help in getting the audio.

Second, I went through the espeak.zip and took out all the cmavo, and
discovered that it's missing 6 gismu. I have no idea which 6, but 6 are
missing.

Because of this, and because it'd be nice to have all the aduio files in the
same .zip, I extracted the complete list of the items for the Smart.fm
lesson into a text file.

The file is located here: http://www.lojban.org/tiki/smart.fm+entry+list

The direct link to the file is:
http://www.lojban.org/tiki/tiki-download_wiki_attachment.php?attId=682&page=smart.fm%20entry%20list&download=y

If Dag or someone could do this one more time, with that list, we won't have
to worry about which 6 gismu are missing.

Thank you in advance.

=== 32 | 2010-03-07T08:52:34Z | "moorkids@juno.com" <moorkids@juno.com> | 20100307.005234.11213.1@webmail02.dca.untd.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267951954.cc24902097dcbbc0.jbomohi:2,S
If you have a list of just gismu you could separate them into different word documents by first letter.  Do a word count for each letter group and compare it with a word count of just that letter gismu from a complete list (like the one here: http://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Index:Lojban/gismu ).  Then narrow down which gismu is missing alphabetically.  (Theirs probably a much faster way to do this but I don't know it)

list all the gismu sound files without .mp3:
  ls | grep '^.....\.mp3' | sed -e 's/.mp3//' > existing.txt
list all the gismu from the gismu list (could've been done easier, i think)
  egrep '^ [a-z]{5}' /usr/share/lojban/gismu.txt | sed -e 's/^ //' -e 's/ .*//'\
  > allgismu.txt
compare the lists:
  diff -n existing.txt allgismu.txt

no output. apparently every gismu is there:

count the number of lines in every textfile:
  wc -l *txt

 1342 allgismu.txt
 1342 existing.txt
 2684 total

=== 34 | 2010-03-07T14:52:46Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003070652x78ae9ce0u5e6d59e806b94ef7@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267973566.2182cbe16ebc1e64.jbomohi:2,S
Okay, then it must be one of the sumti items. Based on my information, there
are 4876 items, which includes the gismu and sumti for place of each gismu.
For example, {klama} accounts for six items- {klama}, {lo klama}, {lo se
klama}, {lo te klama}, {lo ve klama}, and {lo xe klama}.

Subtracting the 1342 gismu from that list leaves 3,534 gismu sumti. The
"gismu_places-espeak.zip" file Dag uploaded to
http://www.lojban.org/tiki/valsi%20Sound%20Files, which contains only gismu
sumti, has 3.527 items, which means either 7 of the items are missing audio,
or the list has 7 extra gismu sumti. I checked my list of the 4786 items,
and it has exactly 1342 gismu, so the error definitely lies somewhere in the
gismu sumti. My mistake.

Timos, would you be so kind as to run that comparison again, but this time
run it against this
list<http://www.lojban.org/tiki/tiki-download_wiki_attachment.php?attId=682&page=smart.fm%20entry%20list&download=y>,
instead of the gismu list?

=== 35 | 2010-03-07T15:22:37Z | Timo Paulssen <timonator@perpetuum-immobile.de> | 4b93c4bd.6090301@perpetuum-immobile.de | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267975357.9bd75267b3be8fbf.jbomohi:2,S
Jonathan Jones wrote:
> Timos, would you be so kind as to run that comparison again, but this time
> run it against this
> list<http://www.lojban.org/tiki/tiki-download_wiki_attachment.php?attId=682&page=smart.fm%20entry%20list&download=y>,

you were right. the following lines are present in one, but not in the other:

lo se rutni
lo te dzena
lo te lanme
lo te mensi
lo te solri
lo te stagi
lo te tamji


the shell commands i used are as follows:

list all sound files:
  ls *mp3 | sed -e 's/.mp3//' | sort > soundedgismu.txt

remove all lines that don't have "lo " at the beginning.
  grep '^lo' smart.fm\ gismu.txt | sort > tobesoundedgismu.txt

show the differences side by side in vim:
  vimdiff soundedgismu.txt tobesoundedgismu.txt

mu'o mi'e la timos noi prami la zedcel

humorous aside: cel means launcher, which is kind of what a shell is used for
(pe'a sai!). very cool :)



=== 36 | 2010-03-07T15:28:23Z | Timo Paulssen <timonator@perpetuum-immobile.de> | 4b93c617.7070408@perpetuum-immobile.de | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267975703.75718cf6fbb9846f.jbomohi:2,S
Timo Paulssen wrote:
> show the differences side by side in vim:
>   vimdiff soundedgismu.txt tobesoundedgismu.txt

after finding out why diff claimed the files were completely different - because
one had unix line endings and the other had windows line endings - and
converting the line endings, this command gives you the difference much more
plainly:

compare the files, show 0 lines of context
  diff -u0 soundedgismu.txt tobesoundedgismu.txt

--- soundedgismu.txt    2010-03-07 16:17:46.620163062 +0100
+++ tobesoundedgismu.txt        2010-03-07 16:23:34.756695081 +0100
@@ -1896,0 +1897 @@
+lo se rutni
@@ -2611,0 +2613 @@
+lo te dzena
@@ -2788,0 +2791 @@
+lo te lanme
@@ -2823,0 +2827 @@
+lo te mensi
@@ -3014,0 +3019 @@
+lo te solri
@@ -3025,0 +3031 @@
+lo te stagi
@@ -3042,0 +3049 @@
+lo te tamji


mu'o mi'e timos

PS: the morale, of course, is, that many more people should learn about shells
than currently do! :)

> lo te stagi

> the shell commands i used are as follows:
>
> list all sound files:
>  ls *mp3 | sed -e 's/.mp3//' | sort > soundedgismu.txt
>
> remove all lines that don't have "lo " at the beginning.
>  grep '^lo' smart.fm\ gismu.txt | sort > tobesoundedgismu.txt
>
> show the differences side by side in vim:
>  vimdiff soundedgismu.txt tobesoundedgismu.txt
>
> mu'o mi'e la timos noi prami la zedcel
>
> humorous aside: cel means launcher, which is kind of what a shell is used
> for
> (pe'a sai!). very cool :)

=== 45 | 2010-03-07T16:01:52Z | Timo Paulssen <timonator@perpetuum-immobile.de> | 4b93cdf0.2060504@perpetuum-immobile.de | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267977712.34a1c0ad29a63fd2.jbomohi:2,S
Jonathan Jones wrote:
> Alright, so if we can get someone to upload the audio for those 7 gismu
> sumti, we'll have the complete set. (And then I can download them, delete
> the cmavo, and combine them all into one archive.)

Another job easily done with one line of script :D

read lines you paste into the terminal and speak&encode and save to the
appropriately named file:

  while read WORDS; do echo $WORDS | espeak -v jbo -w /dev/stdout | lame\
--preset phon+ - "$WORDS".mp3; done
lo se rutni
lo te dzena
lo te lanme
lo te mensi
lo te solri
lo te stagi
lo te tamji

http://www.wakelift.de/lojban/missing_espeak_valsi.zip

unfortunately the format differs from the original files, but i couldn't
immediately figure out what preset would fit and i feared i might lose patience
fiddling with parameters, so whoever wants to tell me, go ahead, or use the line
up there to do it automatically.

lo se rutni.mp3: MPEG ADTS, layer III, v2,  64 kbps, 16 kHz, Monaural
../lo zvati.mp3: MPEG ADTS, layer III, v2,  32 kbps, 22.05 kHz, Monaural

mu'o mi'e timos



=== 46 | 2010-03-07T16:07:09Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003070807p7145378bs95ae86e43b36377c@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267978029.f109fefdf4021639.jbomohi:2,S
This is the bash line Dag used to generate the other audio:

cat gismu_places | while read v; do echo $v; espeak -vjbo --stdout "$v" |
lame -S - "valsi/$v.mp3"; done

Honestly, I'd much rather edit the items BEFORE they are uploaded to
Smart.fm, because that would be easier and much less time consuming.

I doubt you've ever tried to edit an item on Smart.fm, so take my word
for it that it is at least five times as difficult to edit an item
after being uploaded than it is to double-check the items in a
document beforehand- which as far as I'm concerned is the main reason
for the Google doc.

If you can give me the gismu sumti, and some time to double check the
list so that we won't need to worry about editing the items after the
upload, I can fix up the Google doc to be exactly what your script
needs, with all the needed information and formatting.

=== 63 | 2010-03-08T16:17:59Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003080817ia1fbb96y5a9a94720bbc89a1@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1268065079.338e63ebc6185597.jbomohi:2,S
I had to convert it into a Google doc to edit it, due to limitations with
the public computer I'm using. (Not having your own computer sucks, and I
will be VERY happy when I get mine back.)

So far, I've gotten to {lo se fraso}, and your automatic generation leaves a
lot to be desired. That is at least partly my fault.

The gismu sumti all need to have the keyword in their definitions. This way
learners will be able to identify which gismu it is a sumti of, which I
believe should make the gismu sumti easier to memorize, and to prevent
technically correct wrong answers. For instance:

fepni x1 is measured in money subunits as quantity x2 in monetary system x3

The keyword (or phrase, in this case,) is "money subunits". So all the gismu
sumti need to have that.
Your automatic generation produced:

lo fepni a thing measured in money subunits
lo se fepni a quantity of money subunits
lo te fepni a monetary system

{lo te fepni} should be changed to "a monetary system with money subunits"

This is especially important in cases such as the following:

gapru x1 is directly above x2 in reference frame x3
lo gapru something above
lo se gapru something beneath
lo te gapru a reference frame of aboveness

cnita x1 is directly beneath or below x2 in reference frame x3
lo cnita something beneath
lo se cnita something above
lo te cnita a reference frame of beneathness

{lo gapru} = {lo se cnita}, {lo se gapru} = {lo cnita}

If Smart.fm quizzes on {lo gapru}, for instance, and displays the correct
answer for {lo se cnita}, a learners answering with that will be told the
answer is incorrect, even though, technically, it's not.

In this case, {lo se gapru} can be changed to "something to which something
is above", and {lo se cnita} to "something to which something is beneath".
(The "to which" bit isn't absolutely necessary, but "something something
...." sounds odd to me. There may be a better way of phrasing it than I've
put here, but I can't think of it.)

Secondly, many of the definitions are in the form "a thing ...." or "a ...
thing". I would prefer those to be "something ....". "a thing ...." is
easily corrected with a simple find/replace and I've already done that with
the document.

=== 64 | 2010-03-08T16:20:16Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003080820v745c793aqe0fda0a6168e7dba@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1268065216.cce05a487ae2d73d.jbomohi:2,S
Then we have things like this:

lo se fraxu a receive forgivenesser

Which ought to be "a receiver of forgiveness".

On Mon, Mar 8, 2010 at 9:17 AM, Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> wrote:

> I had to convert it into a Google doc to edit it, due to limitations with
> the public computer I'm using. (Not having your own computer sucks, and I
> will be VERY happy when I get mine back.)
>
> So far, I've gotten to {lo se fraso}, and your automatic generation leaves
> a lot to be desired. That is at least partly my fault.
>
> The gismu sumti all need to have the keyword in their definitions. This way
> learners will be able to identify which gismu it is a sumti of, which I
> believe should make the gismu sumti easier to memorize, and to prevent
> technically correct wrong answers. For instance:
>
> fepni x1 is measured in money subunits as quantity x2 in monetary system
> x3
>
> The keyword (or phrase, in this case,) is "money subunits". So all the
> gismu sumti need to have that.
> Your automatic generation produced:
>
> lo fepni a thing measured in money subunits
> lo se fepni a quantity of money subunits
> lo te fepni a monetary system
>
> {lo te fepni} should be changed to "a monetary system with money subunits"
>
> This is especially important in cases such as the following:
>
> gapru x1 is directly above x2 in reference frame x3
> lo gapru something above
> lo se gapru something beneath
> lo te gapru a reference frame of aboveness
>
> cnita x1 is directly beneath or below x2 in reference frame x3
> lo cnita something beneath
> lo se cnita something above
> lo te cnita a reference frame of beneathness
>
> {lo gapru} = {lo se cnita}, {lo se gapru} = {lo cnita}
>
> If Smart.fm quizzes on {lo gapru}, for instance, and displays the correct
> answer for {lo se cnita}, a learners answering with that will be told the
> answer is incorrect, even though, technically, it's not.
>
> In this case, {lo se gapru} can be changed to "something to which something
> is above", and {lo se cnita} to "something to which something is beneath".
> (The "to which" bit isn't absolutely necessary, but "something something
> ...." sounds odd to me. There may be a better way of phrasing it than I've
> put here, but I can't think of it.)
>
> Secondly, many of the definitions are in the form "a thing ...." or "a ...
> thing". I would prefer those to be "something ....". "a thing ...." is
> easily corrected with a simple find/replace and I've already done that with
> the document.
>

On Mon, Mar 8, 2010 at 9:20 AM, Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> wrote:

> Then we have things like this:
>
> lo se fraxu a receive forgivenesser
>
> Which ought to be "a receiver of forgiveness".
>
> On Mon, Mar 8, 2010 at 9:17 AM, Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> wrote:
>
>> I had to convert it into a Google doc to edit it, due to limitations with
>> the public computer I'm using. (Not having your own computer sucks, and I
>> will be VERY happy when I get mine back.)
>>
>> So far, I've gotten to {lo se fraso}, and your automatic generation leaves
>> a lot to be desired. That is at least partly my fault.
>>
>> The gismu sumti all need to have the keyword in their definitions. This
>> way learners will be able to identify which gismu it is a sumti of, which I
>> believe should make the gismu sumti easier to memorize, and to prevent
>> technically correct wrong answers. For instance:
>>
>> fepni x1 is measured in money subunits as quantity x2 in monetary system
>> x3
>>
>> The keyword (or phrase, in this case,) is "money subunits". So all the
>> gismu sumti need to have that.
>> Your automatic generation produced:
>>
>> lo fepni a thing measured in money subunits
>> lo se fepni a quantity of money subunits
>> lo te fepni a monetary system
>>
>> {lo te fepni} should be changed to "a monetary system with money subunits"
>>
>> This is especially important in cases such as the following:
>>
>> gapru x1 is directly above x2 in reference frame x3
>> lo gapru something above
>> lo se gapru something beneath
>> lo te gapru a reference frame of aboveness
>>
>> cnita x1 is directly beneath or below x2 in reference frame x3
>> lo cnita something beneath
>> lo se cnita something above
>> lo te cnita a reference frame of beneathness
>>
>> {lo gapru} = {lo se cnita}, {lo se gapru} = {lo cnita}
>>
>> If Smart.fm quizzes on {lo gapru}, for instance, and displays the correct
>> answer for {lo se cnita}, a learners answering with that will be told the
>> answer is incorrect, even though, technically, it's not.
>>
>> In this case, {lo se gapru} can be changed to "something to which
>> something is above", and {lo se cnita} to "something to which something is
>> beneath". (The "to which" bit isn't absolutely necessary, but "something
>> something ...." sounds odd to me. There may be a better way of phrasing it
>> than I've put here, but I can't think of it.)
>>
>> Secondly, many of the definitions are in the form "a thing ...." or "a ...
>> thing". I would prefer those to be "something ....". "a thing ...." is
>> easily corrected with a simple find/replace and I've already done that with
>> the document.
>>

=== 66 | 2010-03-10T14:43:57Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003100643g39563acagba9f4b9c25d6a10b@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1268232237.c8fa5dcceb254551.jbomohi:2,S
I just wanted to make sure that you were aware of something, as I'm not
certain that you are.

There are actually three lessons on Smart.fm for the items:

The all-inclusive lesson, which is to contain all the items:
http://smart.fm/goals/179543
<http://smart.fm/goals/179543>The place-structure lesson, which to contain
the bare gismu: http://smart.fm/goals/179606
<http://smart.fm/goals/179606>And the sumti lesson, which is to contain the
gismu sumti: http://smart.fm/goals/179607

<http://smart.fm/goals/179607>So, when you do the uploading, I ask that you
please upload the appropriate items to the other two lessons as well.

Also, do you want the third (part of speech) and fourth (.mp3 filename)
columns when I send you the list back? I've used the conventions of calling
gismu "Phrase" (E) and gismu sumti "Noun" (N) in order to make it easier to
separate them from each other for inclusion into the other lesson they
respectively go into. If not, I can easily delete those columns from the
document.

> I just wanted to make sure that you were aware of something, as I'm not

>
> There are actually three lessons on Smart.fm for the items:
>
> The all-inclusive lesson, which is to contain all the items:
> http://smart.fm/goals/179543
>  <http://smart.fm/goals/179543>The place-structure lesson, which to
> contain the bare gismu: http://smart.fm/goals/179606
> <http://smart.fm/goals/179606>And the sumti lesson, which is to contain
> the gismu sumti: http://smart.fm/goals/179607
>
> <http://smart.fm/goals/179607>So, when you do the uploading, I ask that
> you please upload the appropriate items to the other two lessons as well.
>
> Also, do you want the third (part of speech) and fourth (.mp3 filename)
> columns when I send you the list back? I've used the conventions of calling
> gismu "Phrase" (E) and gismu sumti "Noun" (N) in order to make it easier to
> separate them from each other for inclusion into the other lesson they
> respectively go into. If not, I can easily delete those columns from the
> document.
>

just silly.

Looking at your script, the 5th argument is the list name. You could try
using "Lojban gismu", the name of the all-inclusive goal,
http://smart.fm/goals/179543. Alternatively, you may be able to adjust arg4

> I did tell you about the most prevalent pattern: "a(n) ... thing", and the
desired alteration: "something..."

As in: "a drunk thing" -> "something drunk"

Another thing I've seen a lot of is too many "e"s. As in "a useer", which I
take to be "a use-er" with the dash taken out.

And another one I mentioned:

"an express commenter" -> "an expresser of a comment"

And:

"a leap destination" -> "a destination  of a leap"
"a leap origin" -> "an origin of a leap"
"a jump propulsion" -> "a propulsion of a jump"
 (Although this one I don't think can be done by computer, however, "a ...
standard" -> "a standard of ..." ought to be.)

I've gone through ~80% of the list already. All of the gismu have been
double-checked, and all of the gismu sumti to {lo se rirni} as well, so at
this point all that remains is the gismu sumti from {lo rirxe}  on.

In two (three?) days effort I've managed to get all but 1001 of 4876 items
checked and edited, so I figure one more session and I'll be finished
anyway, especially since most of the items don't need to be edited. I'll
probably be able to knock out the last few after lunch, or at the least
before lunch tomorrow.

>      >    expresser of a�comment"
>      >
>      >    however, "a ... standard" -> "a standard of ..." ought to be.)
>

Honestly, I have absolutely no qualms whatsoever with you deleting the items
in the list currently, provided the upload works.

Of course, once you know for certain that things *do* work, I would much
rather you upload to the existing lists than to create new ones- partly
because the people currently subscribed to learn the lesson won't have to
find the new lesson, partly because the guy putting the news on
lojban.orgwon't have to find out what the address is for a new lesson,
and mostly
because of stubborn pride. ;)

is tavla).

Go to the goal (Or to your created items, which will make it easier to sort
them (as sorting doesn't seem to actually work in Goal Content), click the
item(s) you want to delete- selected items will become highlighted. Scroll
to the bottom of the page and click the trash can icon. You may be asked for
confirmation, and will recieve a message stating the items were deleted.


> > What about cmavo?  Just take the definitions as they currently are
> > in the list?
>
> Still open.
>

You could do that, certainly. It'd be nice to have individual entries for
each point on the scale for scalar cmavo (i.e. {.ui}, {.uicai}, {.uinai},
eic.), but I think that it'd be too cumbersome to do that, and learners more
than likely could figure it out. In short, I think a striaght one to one
transfer from the cmavo list would be fine.

The only (big) question in my mind is, how to order them? Alphabetical is
certainly not the way to do it, but both selma'o order and usage order have
their points.

change, if you want to change them back:

cusku x1 says x2 for audience x3 via medium x4
lo cusku a sayer
lo se cusku something said
lo te cusku an audience of something said
lo ve cusku a medium for something said

=== 97 | 2010-03-07T17:56:04Z | Jonathan Jones <eyeonus@gmail.com> | 702226df1003070956w568d0af5hf913ba71089abd26@mail.gmail.com | mail/lojban-list/cur/1267984564.35c64430b770a878.jbomohi:2,S
One per line, tab separated, so it's easy to copy/paste.

i.e.:
bajra  x1 runs on x2 using limbs x3 with gait x4
lo bajra a runner
lo se bajra a running surface
lo te bajra limbs runs with
lo ve bajra a running gait
bakfu  x1 is a bundle or pack containing x2 held together by x3
lo bakfu a package
lo se bakfu contents of a pack
lo te bakfu what is holding a pack together

You can skip the gismu definitions, of course. If you do, leave a blank line
between:

....
lo ve bajra a running gait

lo bakfu a package
....
